<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that planar first-passage percolation with independent identically distributed nonnegative nonatomic edge weights has almost surely no doubly infinite geodesic, provided the minimum of four independent weights has finite second moment. This resolves the planar no-bigeodesics conjecture under that moment assumption. The conclusion rules out all bigeodesics simultaneously, without any regularity assumption on the limit shape.

- **claim-no-bigeodesics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Suppose that $G$ has no atoms and that, for independent $X_1,X_2,X_3,X_4$ with law $G$, $$\label{eq:minimum-moment} \mathbb E\!\left[\min\{X_1,X_2,X_3,X_4\}^{\,2}\right]<\infty.$$ Then, with probability one, there is no sequence $(v_i)_{i\in\mathbb Z}$ of pairwise distinct vertices with consecutive vertices adjacent and $$\sum_{k=i}^{j-1}t_{\{v_k,v_{k+1}\}}=T(v_i,v_j) \qquad\text{for every }i<j.$$
  - Explanation: Main theorem. For iid nonatomic weights whose minimum of four copies has finite second moment, almost surely no doubly infinite geodesic exists, and all bigeodesics are excluded on one event with no regularity of the limit shape assumed.
- **claim-finite-geodesic-unique** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-minimizers)): Almost surely every pair of vertices has a unique finite geodesic.
  - Explanation: Almost surely only finitely many simple paths from a vertex stay under any cost level, and continuous weights make ties between distinct paths a null event, so finite geodesics exist and are unique.
- **claim-busemann-basic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-busemann-basic)): For every ray $p$ and all vertices $x,y$, the limit $$\label{eq:busemann-definition} B_p(x,y)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\bigl(T(x,p_j)-T(y,p_j)\bigr)$$ exists. It is additive and antisymmetric, satisfies $\lvert B_p(x,y)\rvert\le T(x,y)$, and is calibrated on the ray: $$B_p(p_i,p_j)=T(p_i,p_j)\qquad(i\le j).$$ Deleting a finite initial portion of a ray does not change its Busemann function. Coalescing rays have the same Busemann function. For each fixed vertex $x$, $$\label{eq:calibration-defect} 0\le T(x,p_j)-B_p(x,p_j)\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Every ray has a Busemann function defined as a limit of passage-time differences. It is additive, antisymmetric, dominated by the metric, calibrated along the ray, unchanged by deleting an initial piece, and equal for coalescing rays.
- **claim-structure-theorem** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-structure)): There are a deterministic norm $m$ on $\mathbb R^2$ and a deterministic closed subset $C\subseteq\mathcal S$ with the following properties.* 1. *Almost surely, $$T(0,x)=m(x)+o(\|x\|)\qquad (\|x\|\to\infty).$$* 2. *Almost surely, simultaneously for all rays $p$ from all lattice vertices, there is a unique $\rho_p\in C$ such that $$\label{eq:busemann-linearity} B_p(0,x)=\rho_p(x)+o(\|x\|)\qquad (\|x\|\to\infty).$$ The limiting directions of $p$ lie in $A(\rho_p)$. At each vertex the set of realized functionals is $C$.* 3. *For each fixed $\rho\in C$, almost surely there is exactly one ray $g_\rho(x)$ with functional $\rho$ from each $x\in\mathbb Z^2$. These rays coalesce. Their family is *backwards finite*: for each vertex $z$, the set of $x$ for which $g_\rho(x)$ contains $z$ is finite.* *The norm $m$ and the set $C$ have the rotation and reflection symmetries of the square lattice.
  - Explanation: Structural input from the Ahlberg–Hoffman theory together with the shape theorem: a deterministic norm and closed set of functionals, simultaneous asymptotic linearity of all rays, and coalescence with backward finiteness for each fixed functional.
- **claim-fixed-label-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-fixed-label)): For each fixed $\rho\in C$, almost surely no bigeodesic has an end with label $\rho$. The same holds simultaneously for every member of any countable deterministic subset of $C$.
  - Explanation: A bigeodesic with an end of a fixed label would put its middle vertex in the family of that label from infinitely many starting vertices, contradicting backward finiteness. The statement extends to countable deterministic label sets.
- **claim-uniform-metric** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-metric)): Almost surely, $$\label{eq:uniform-metric} T(x,y)=m(y-x)+o(\|x\|+\|y\|) \qquad (\|x\|+\|y\|\to\infty),$$ uniformly over the two endpoints.
  - Explanation: The passage time between two far points is approximated by the limit norm of their displacement with sublinear error, uniformly over both endpoints.
- **claim-finite-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-change)): Suppose two environments differ on a finite set $F$ of edges, and put $K=\sum_{e\in F}\|t'_e-t_e\|$. Then $$\|T'(x,y)-T(x,y)\|\le K\qquad(x,y\in\mathbb Z^2).$$ If a path is a ray in both environments and has asymptotically linear Busemann functions in both, its label is the same in the two environments. The conclusion also applies when a common tail is a ray in both. If $t'_e\ge t_e$ for all $e$ and a geodesic path uses only edges whose weights are unchanged, it remains geodesic in the new metric.
  - Explanation: Changing finitely many edge weights shifts every passage time by at most the total change, preserves labels of retained rays, and keeps a geodesic that avoids changed edges geodesic after an increase.
- **claim-measurability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-measurable)): The conditions that a specified path is a ray or a bigeodesic, that specified rays coalesce, and that a ray’s Busemann function is asymptotically linear to a specified functional are Borel conditions on the corresponding environment, path, and functional spaces. Existence events obtained by projecting these conditions, with additional Borel tests, are analytic and hence universally measurable. On such existence events a witnessing path can be selected completion-measurably. The number of distinct labels with such witnesses is measurable in the completed probability space, with the value $+\infty$ allowed.
  - Explanation: Ray, bigeodesic, coalescence and asymptotic-linearity conditions are Borel, so existence events are analytic and witnesses can be selected measurably in the completed space.
- **claim-opposite-functionals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-opposite)): Let an oriented bigeodesic pass through $z$, and let $p^+$ and $p^-$ be its two rays starting at $z$. Then, for every $w\in\mathbb Z^2$, $$\label{eq:bigeodesic-sum} B_{p^+}(z,w)+B_{p^-}(z,w)\leq 0.$$ Consequently $\rho_{p^-}=-\rho_{p^+}$. Moving the base point along the bigeodesic does not change either functional or either Busemann function. For any deterministic countable set $D\subset C$, almost surely no bigeodesic has an end whose functional belongs to $D$.
  - Explanation: The two arms of a bigeodesic have opposite labels and Busemann sums bounded by zero. The base point can move along the bigeodesic, and a deterministic countable label set is avoided by every bigeodesic end.
- **claim-countable-charts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-charts)): There is a deterministic countable set $D\subset C$, symmetric under negation, and a countable family of charts with the following properties. Each chart is specified by a choice of lattice coordinates and a compact arc $J$ of the dual unit circle, whose distinct endpoints $\rho_\ell, \rho_r$ belong to $C$. Set $I=C\cap J$. There is $c>0$ such that $$\label{eq:chart-contact-sector} d_2\geq c \quad\text{for every }\rho\in J\text{ and every }d\in A(\rho).$$ The coordinate $$r(\rho)=\rho(e_1)$$ is one-to-one on $J$ and orders it from $\rho_\ell$ to $\rho_r$ with $r(\rho_\ell)<r(\rho_r)$. Every member of $C\setminus D$ belongs to the relative interior of such a chart arc. Almost surely, every bigeodesic can therefore be oriented so that its upper functional belongs to the interior of one of these charts and its lower functional is its negative.
  - Explanation: The possible ends of a bigeodesic reduce to countably many charts, each a compact arc of the dual circle on which the horizontal coordinate orders the labels and contact directions point strictly upward.
- **claim-local-ray-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ray-order)): Two upper rays with disjoint tails have a stable left-to-right order at their first visits to the horizontal rows $\{x_2=N\}$ as $N\to\infty$. If $p$ is to the left of $q$ in this order, then $$\label{eq:ray-order} r(\rho_p)\leq r(\rho_q).$$ In particular, distinct labels give disjoint tails and strictly ordered first arrivals, in the order of increasing $r$. For lower rays the same statements hold as $N\to-\infty$, again with left-to-right order given by increasing horizontal coordinate of the lower functional.
  - Explanation: Upper rays with disjoint tails have a stable left-to-right order on distant rows, and that order is compatible with the order of the horizontal coordinates of their labels; distinct labels give disjoint tails.
- **claim-uniform-cone-confinement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cone)): Almost surely there is a finite $H(z)\geq1$ for every $z\in\mathbb Z^2$ such that every upper ray from $z$ lies in $Q^+(z,H(z))$ and every lower ray from $z$ lies in $Q^-(z,H(z))$. Moreover, for every fixed $L<\infty$, $$\label{eq:uniform-cone-offset} \max_{\|z\|\leq Ln}H(z)=o(n)\qquad(n\to\infty).$$ These statements hold simultaneously for every ray in the chart; no uniform rate of Busemann convergence over the intermediate labels is required.
  - Explanation: All upper and lower rays from every vertex lie in offset cones, with offsets growing sublinearly on balls, by trapping intermediate rays between four fixed bounding rays through a Jordan curve argument.
- **claim-crossing-separated-sites** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-crossing)): Let $x_1<y_1$, and suppose upper rays $p$ from $x$ and $q$ from $y$ satisfy $$p\subset Q^+(x,H_x),\qquad q\subset Q^+(y,H_y).$$ If $$\label{eq:crossing-separation} s(y_1-x_1)>H_x+H_y+\|x_2-y_2\|$$ and $r(\rho_p)>r(\rho_q)$, then the rays intersect. The same assertion holds for two lower rays in the corresponding lower cones, with the horizontal coordinates of their lower labels in place of $r(\rho_p)$ and $r(\rho_q)$.
  - Explanation: If two sites are far enough apart compared with the cone offsets and the labels are in reversed order, the two rays must intersect.
- **claim-calibrated-rays** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-calibrated-rays)): Let $p$ be a ray, let $B=B_p$, and let $x\in\mathbb Z^2$. There is a ray $q=(q_j)_{j\ge0}$ from $x$ such that $$T(x,q_j)=B(x,q_j)\qquad(j\ge0). \label{eq:calibrated-from-x}$$ Moreover, $q$ has the same label as $p$. In general the conclusion concerns the labels; it does not assert $B_q=B_p$.
  - Explanation: From any vertex there is a ray calibrated by the Busemann function of a given ray and carrying the same label, although its own Busemann function need not coincide.
- **claim-exceptional-countable** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-exceptional-countable)): Almost surely $\mathcal E_I(\omega)$ is at most countable. The same conclusion holds with $I$ replaced by $-I$.
  - Explanation: Labels with branching rays or with differing Busemann functions form an at most countable set, shown by a planar word argument for branching and by disjoint value intervals for Busemann disagreement.
- **claim-independent-exceptional** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-independent-exceptional)): Let $V,W$ be independent environments, each with the iid law $\mathbb P$. Almost surely, simultaneously for every $\rho\in\mathcal E_I(V)$, all $W$-rays of label $-\rho$ coalesce.
  - Explanation: For two independent environments, the exceptional labels of one are an ordinary countable parameter set for the other, so the rays of the opposite label coalesce, via an analytic set and a Fubini argument.
- **claim-half-plane-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cut-transfer)): There is a deterministic increasing sequence $n_k\to\infty$ and an event of probability one for the coupling <a href="#eq:half-plane-coupling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:half-plane-coupling">[eq:half-plane-coupling]</a> with the following properties.* 1. *For every finite collection of upper $U$-rays and every finite set $K\subset\mathbb Z^2$, all sufficiently large $k$ have the following property. Every ray $p$ in that collection is a $V_{n_k}$-ray with exactly its original label, and $$B_p^{V_{n_k}}(x,y)=B_p^U(x,y)\qquad(x,y\in K).$$ More precisely, for each such $k$ and $p$, the distances $T_{V_{n_k}}(x,p_j)$ and $T_U(x,p_j)$ are equal for all sufficiently large $j$, simultaneously for $x\in K$.* 2. *For each fixed $n\ge1$, every lower $U$-ray has a tail which is a $W_n$-ray with exactly its original label.* *The thresholds may depend on the rays, their labels, $K$, and, in part (ii), on $n$.
  - Explanation: Along a deterministic subsequence of horizontal cuts, upper rays keep their exact labels and finite Busemann values in one coupled environment, and lower rays have tails with their exact labels in the other.
- **claim-opposite-coalescence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-opposite-coalescence)): Almost surely, simultaneously for all $\rho\in\mathcal E_I(U)$, every two $U$-rays of label $-\rho$ coalesce. Almost surely the same statement holds with upper and lower sectors exchanged. These conclusions hold simultaneously for the countable collection of charts under consideration.
  - Explanation: In one environment, at every exceptional label, all rays of the opposite label coalesce, obtained by transferring witnesses across a cut and using the independent-environment result.
- **claim-budget-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-budget-comparison)): Let $T$ be a base metric and $T^k$ a metric obtained by increasing finitely many edge weights. Both environments are assumed to have the structural properties already established. Suppose that $k,y$ lie on the same horizontal line, with $k_1<y_1$, and that the following conditions hold for a bin $(\alpha,\beta)_I$.* 1. *Every changed edge has both endpoints in $\{z:\|z-k\|_\infty\le R\}$, where $R\ge1$.* 2. *In $T^k$ there is an oriented bigeodesic through $k$ with upper label $\rho\in(\alpha,\beta)_I$. Its arms $p^+$ and $p^-$ are contained in $Q^+(k,H_p)$ and $Q^-(k,H_p)$, respectively.* 3. *Finite initial portions of these two arms have all their vertices in the same ball of radius $R$, and the sum of the increases along those portions is at least $b>0$.* 4. *The base rays from $y$ of labels $\alpha$ and $-\beta$ are contained in $Q^+(y,H_c)$ and $Q^-(y,H_c)$, respectively.* *There is a separation $S=S(s,R,H_p,H_c)$ such that $y_1-k_1>S$ implies $$\label{eq:budget-comparison} b\le B_\alpha(y,k)+B_{-\beta}(y,k).$$ One may take $$\label{eq:budget-separation} S=\max\left\{R+\frac{R+H_c}{s},\frac{H_p+H_c}{s}\right\} \le C_s(1+R+H_p+H_c),$$ with $C_s$ depending only on $s$. In particular, the choice of $S$ is uniform over bins and does not depend on the magnitudes of the individual increases.
  - Explanation: After a finite increase, a marked bigeodesic's added cost is bounded by the sum of two fixed-label Busemann increments from a sufficiently separated site, with a separation independent of the bin and of the size of the increases.
- **claim-label-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-label-budget)): Fix a bin $(\alpha,\beta)_I$, an integer spacing $l\ge1$, and a gain $b>0$. On a common probability space, mark some sites of the horizontal grid $k_j=(jl,0)$, $j\in\mathbb Z$. Suppose all sites have the same marginal probability $q_{\alpha,\beta}$ of being marked in this bin, and the base environment has its usual fixed-label Busemann asymptotics. Suppose also that for successive marks $k<y$ in the bin the comparison <a href="#eq:budget-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:budget-comparison">[eq:budget-comparison]</a> holds. In particular, this is so if the hypotheses of [lem:budget-comparison](#lem:budget-comparison) hold with common deterministic bounds and successive marks are sufficiently separated. Then $$\label{eq:label-budget} \frac{b}{l}\,q_{\alpha,\beta} \le r(\beta)-r(\alpha).$$ No independence or ergodicity assumption on the marks is needed.
  - Explanation: Gain per unit of grid spacing times the marking probability is at most the width of the bin in the label coordinate, with no independence or ergodicity of marks required.
- **claim-usable-edges** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-usable)): There exist $a_0<b_0$ such that $$\label{eq:low-usable-probabilities} \begin{gathered} p_a=\mathbb P(t_e\le a_0)>0, \qquad q=\mathbb P(t_e<b_0)<\frac1{64},\\ p_b=\mathbb P(t_e\ge b_0)=1-q>0. \end{gathered}$$ Call an edge *usable* if $t_e\ge b_0$, and put $\theta=8\sqrt q<1$. For each vertex $x$ and integer $n_0\ge1$, $$\label{eq:usable-fixed-site} \mathbb P\left( \begin{array}{c} \text{some simple path from $x$ of length $j\ge n_0$}\\ \text{has fewer than $j/2$ usable edges} \end{array}\right) \le\frac{\theta^{n_0}}{1-\theta}.$$ If $U_n$ is the event that every simple path starting in $[-3n,3n]^2\cap\mathbb Z^2$ and having length at least $n/2$ has at least half its edges usable, then $$\label{eq:usable-box} \mathbb P(U_n^c) \le (6n+1)^2\frac{\theta^{\lceil n/2\rceil}}{1-\theta} \longrightarrow0.$$ Almost surely, at every fixed vertex all sufficiently long simple paths satisfy the same fraction bound.
  - Explanation: Thresholds exist such that long simple paths have at least half of their edges above the upper threshold, with a geometric failure probability and no moment of an edge weight needed.
- **claim-edge-label-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-edge-count)): There is a finite constant $C$, depending only on the chart and the weight law, such that for every lattice edge $e$ and every $H\ge1$, $$\label{eq:edge-label-count} \mathbb EN_e(H)\le C(H+1).$$
  - Explanation: The expected number of distinct bigeodesic labels through a given edge, under cone regularity with offset H, grows at most linearly in H, by a one-edge resampling mark and the label budget.
- **claim-no-forks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-no-forks)): Almost surely there is no upper or lower fork in any of the countably many charts under consideration.
  - Explanation: A positive fork density would force a cubic expected number of usable edge-label pairs in large boxes, contradicting the much smaller bound from the edge count, so no forks exist.
- **claim-common-busemann-pair** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-same-busemann)): On one event of probability one, whenever two oriented bigeodesics $p,q$ in any fixed chart have the same upper label, their two Busemann functions agree: $$\label{eq:same-busemann-pair} B_{p^+}=B_{q^+},\qquad B_{p^-}=B_{q^-}.$$ This assertion is simultaneous over the bigeodesics and their possibly random labels. It also holds almost surely in every environment whose law is absolutely continuous with respect to the iid law, and simultaneously for a countable collection of such environments.
  - Explanation: Two bigeodesics with the same upper label have equal upper and equal lower Busemann functions; otherwise opposite coalescence would produce a forbidden fork. The conclusion transfers to absolutely continuous environments.
- **claim-uniform-change-of-law** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-likelihood)): Every individual environment $Y^{k,R}$ and every union environment $Y^{k,y,R}$ has law absolutely continuous with respect to $\mu$. There is a finite constant $C$, depending only on $s,H_0,p_a,p_b$, such that the density $L_{k,R}=d\mathcal L(Y^{k,R})/d\mu$ satisfies $$\label{eq:likelihood-bound} \mathbb E_\mu L_{k,R}^2\leq\exp(C\eta^2),\qquad \\|\mathcal L(Y^{k,R})-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \leq\tfrac12\sqrt{\exp(C\eta^2)-1}.$$ The bounds are uniform in $k$ and $R$.
  - Explanation: The low-to-high replacement environments are absolutely continuous with respect to the base law, with uniformly bounded second moment of the density and controlled total variation distance.
- **claim-posterior-bernoulli** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-posterior)): Fix $k,R$. Conditional on the entire individual environment $Y^{k,R}$, the variables $(\xi_e:e\in A_k(R))$ are independent Bernoulli variables with respective success probabilities $$\label{eq:posterior} \theta_e(Y^{k,R}_e) =\mathbf1_{[b_0,\infty)}(Y^{k,R}_e) \frac{\delta_{h(e)}}{p_b+\delta_{h(e)}}.$$ The assertion includes conditioning on every unchanged exterior edge. If a simple path is selected measurably from this individual output alone, the same product law holds on any of its finite prefixes lying in $A_k(R)$, once that prefix is fixed by the conditioning.
  - Explanation: Given the full modified environment, the latent raise indicators are independent Bernoulli variables supported on weights above the upper threshold, also along a prefix of a path selected from that output.
- **claim-divergent-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-divergent-gain)): There is a fixed integer $R_0\geq n_0$ such that, for each positive integer $M$, one can choose a deterministic $R(M)\geq R_0$ with the following property. On $\mathcal E_{k,R(M)}$, conditional on $Y^{k,R(M)}$, with probability at least $3/4$ the selected upper arm has both a strict raise among its first $R_0$ edges and total increase at least $M$ along its first $R(M)$ edges.
  - Explanation: Because the replacement probabilities have a divergent sum along a path with many usable edges, a radius can be chosen so that a raise near the root and an arbitrarily large total toll both hold with high conditional probability.
- **claim-tilt-marks** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-tilt-marks)): With $R_0$ fixed as above, for every positive integer $M$ the common coupling defines marks $J_M(k)\in\{0,1\}$ at all horizontal sites, with the same marginal probability at each site and $$\label{eq:initial-mark-probability} \mathbb P(J_M(k)=1)\geq p/4.$$ A mark selects a bigeodesic $p_k$ in $T^{k,R(M)}$ through $k$, with upper label $\rho_k\in I$ avoiding all partition endpoints. Its arms lie in $Q^\pm(k,H_0)$. Its first $R(M)$ upper-arm edges have total increase at least $M$ over the base environment, and one of its first $R_0$ upper-arm edges has increase at least $g$. All changes occur in a deterministic bounded neighborhood of $k$ whose radius may depend on $M$.
  - Explanation: A coupled family of marks on horizontal sites with equal marginal probability bounded below selects bigeodesics in finitely raised environments with arbitrarily large total toll and a raised edge near the site.
- **claim-union-preservation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-union)): Fix $M$, put $R=R(M)$, and suppose distinct sites $k,y\in\Lambda$ are marked. Their selected paths $p_k,p_y$ are bigeodesics in the union metric $T^*=T^{k,y,R}$. Every edge of $p_k$ has the same weight there as in $T^{k,R}$, and every edge of $p_y$ has the same weight there as in $T^{y,R}$. Their labels are preserved. Moreover, any raised edge among the first $R_0$ upper-arm edges of $p_y$ lies outside $A_k(R)$ and has its base weight in $T^{k,R}$.
  - Explanation: For two marked sites on the coarse grid, both selected paths remain bigeodesics with preserved labels in the union environment, and the near raised edge of one lies outside the region of the other.
- **claim-distinct-marked-labels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-distinct-marks)): Almost surely, for each positive integer $M$, the selected upper labels at distinct marked sites of $\Lambda$ are distinct.
  - Explanation: If two marked sites shared a label, equal Busemann pairs would give a detour through a strictly raised edge that is cheaper than a selected geodesic in the individual metric, a contradiction.
- **claim-boundary-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-correction)): For $x\in H\cap\mathbb Z^2$, the limit $$D_{\mathcal G}(x) =\lim_{j\to\infty}\bigl(T_H(x,v_j)-T(x,v_j)\bigr)$$ exists and is finite and nonnegative, where $(v_j)$ is any ray of $\mathcal G$ and the targets are sufficiently far along its $H$-contained tail. The limit is independent of the target ray. The variables $D_{\mathcal G}(kz)$, $k\in\mathbb Z$, are stationary under boundary translations, and the restricted Busemann limit satisfies $$\label{eq:boundary-correction} B_{\mathcal G}^H(x,y) =B_{\mathcal G}(x,y)+D_{\mathcal G}(x)-D_{\mathcal G}(y).$$
  - Explanation: The difference between half-plane and full-plane distances to a ray's targets converges to a finite nonnegative correction that is stationary along the boundary and relates the restricted and full Busemann functions.
- **claim-zero-boundary-difference** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-zero-boundary-difference)): Let $\Delta(k,l)$, $k,l\in\mathbb Z$, be a finite, additive stationary process, with $\Delta(k,k+1)\geq0$ almost surely. If $\Delta(0,n)/n\to0$ in probability, then every increment is zero almost surely.
  - Explanation: A nonnegative additive stationary process whose growth over n steps divided by n tends to zero in probability has every increment equal to zero, with no integrability or ergodicity needed.
- **claim-boundary-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-uniqueness)): Two random coalescing geodesics with the same full-plane Busemann linear functional agree almost surely under the assumptions of [thm:main](#thm:main).
  - Explanation: Two random coalescing geodesic families with the same full-plane functional agree, using finite boundary corrections and the vanishing lemma in place of a boundary expectation step.

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-change",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose two environments differ on a finite set $F$ of edges, and put $K=\\sum_{e\\in F}|t'_e-t_e|$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-measurable",
        "exact_quote": "The conditions that a specified path is a ray or a bigeodesic, that specified rays coalesce, and that a ray’s Busemann function is asymptotically linear to a specified functional are Borel conditions on the corresponding environment, path, and functional spaces. Existence events obtained by projecting these conditions, with additional Borel tests, are analytic and hence universally measurable. On such existence events a witnessing path can be selected completion-measurably. The number of distinct labels with such witnesses is measurable in the completed probability space, with the value $+\\infty$ allowed.",
        "source_id": "PM-212.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-measurable",

On such existence events a witnessing path can be selected completion-measurably.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-opposite",
        "exact_quote": "Let an oriented bigeodesic pass through $z$, and let $p^+$ and $p^-$ be its two rays starting at $z$. Then, for every $w\\in\\mathbb Z^2$, $$\\label{eq:bigeodesic-sum}\n B_{p^+}(z,w)+B_{p^-}(z,w)\\leq 0.$$ Consequently $\\rho_{p^-}=-\\rho_{p^+}$. Moving the base point along the bigeodesic does not change either functional or either Busemann function. For any deterministic countable set $D\\subset C$, almost surely no bigeodesic has an end whose functional belongs to $D$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-charts",
        "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic countable set $D\\subset C$, symmetric under negation, and a countable family of charts with the following properties. Each chart is specified by a choice of lattice coordinates and a compact arc $J$ of the dual unit circle, whose distinct endpoints $\\rho_\\ell,\n\\rho_r$ belong to $C$. Set $I=C\\cap J$. There is $c>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:chart-contact-sector}\n d_2\\geq c\n \\quad\\text{for every }\\rho\\in J\\text{ and every }d\\in A(\\rho).$$ The coordinate $$r(\\rho)=\\rho(e_1)$$ is one-to-one on $J$ and orders it from $\\rho_\\ell$ to $\\rho_r$ with $r(\\rho_\\ell)<r(\\rho_r)$. Every member of $C\\setminus D$ belongs to the relative interior of such a chart arc. Almost surely, every bigeodesic can therefore be oriented so that its upper functional belongs to the interior of one of these charts and its lower functional is its negative.",
        "source_id": "PM-212.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cut-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic increasing sequence $n_k\\to\\infty$ and an event of probability one for the coupling <a href=\"#eq:half-plane-coupling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:half-plane-coupling\">[eq:half-plane-coupling]</a> with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every finite collection of upper $U$-rays and every finite set $K\\subset\\mathbb Z^2$, all sufficiently large $k$ have the following property. Every ray $p$ in that collection is a $V_{n_k}$-ray with exactly its original label, and $$B_p^{V_{n_k}}(x,y)=B_p^U(x,y)\\qquad(x,y\\in K).$$ More precisely, for each such $k$ and $p$, the distances $T_{V_{n_k}}(x,p_j)$ and $T_U(x,p_j)$ are equal for all sufficiently large $j$, simultaneously for $x\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *For each fixed $n\\ge1$, every lower $U$-ray has a tail which is a $W_n$-ray with exactly its original label.*\n\n*The thresholds may depend on the rays, their labels, $K$, and, in part (ii), on $n$.",

      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cut-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic increasing sequence $n_k\\to\\infty$ and an event of probability one for the coupling <a href=\"#eq:half-plane-coupling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:half-plane-coupling\">[eq:half-plane-coupling]</a> with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every finite collection of upper $U$-rays and every finite set $K\\subset\\mathbb Z^2$, all sufficiently large $k$ have the following property. Every ray $p$ in that collection is a $V_{n_k}$-ray with exactly its original label, and $$B_p^{V_{n_k}}(x,y)=B_p^U(x,y)\\qquad(x,y\\in K).$$ More precisely, for each such $k$ and $p$, the distances $T_{V_{n_k}}(x,p_j)$ and $T_U(x,p_j)$ are equal for all sufficiently large $j$, simultaneously for $x\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *For each fixed $n\\ge1$, every lower $U$-ray has a tail which is a $W_n$-ray with exactly its original label.*\n\n*The thresholds may depend on the rays, their labels, $K$, and, in part (ii), on $n$.",

      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a deterministic increasing sequence $n_k\\to\\infty$ and an event of probability one for the coupling <a href=\"#eq:half-plane-coupling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:half-plane-coupling\">[eq:half-plane-coupling]</a> with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every finite collection of upper $U$-rays and every finite set $K\\subset\\mathbb Z^2$, all sufficiently large $k$ have the following property. Every ray $p$ in that collection is a $V_{n_k}$-ray with exactly its original label, and $$B_p^{V_{n_k}}(x,y)=B_p^U(x,y)\\qquad(x,y\\in K).$$ More precisely, for each such $k$ and $p$, the distances $T_{V_{n_k}}(x,p_j)$ and $T_U(x,p_j)$ are equal for all sufficiently large $j$, simultaneously for $x\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *For each fixed $n\\ge1$, every lower $U$-ray has a tail which is a $W_n$-ray with exactly its original label.*\n\n*The thresholds may depend on the rays, their labels, $K$, and, in part (ii), on $n$.",
      "explanation": "Along a deterministic subsequence of horizontal cuts, upper rays keep their exact labels and finite Busemann values in one coupled environment, and lower rays have tails with their exact labels in the other.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-budget-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a base metric and $T^k$ a metric obtained by increasing finitely many edge weights. Both environments are assumed to have the structural properties already established. Suppose that $k,y$ lie on the same horizontal line, with $k_1<y_1$, and that the following conditions hold for a bin $(\\alpha,\\beta)_I$.*\n\n1.  *Every changed edge has both endpoints in $\\{z:|z-k|_\\infty\\le R\\}$, where $R\\ge1$.*\n\n2.  *In $T^k$ there is an oriented bigeodesic through $k$ with upper label $\\rho\\in(\\alpha,\\beta)_I$. Its arms $p^+$ and $p^-$ are contained in $Q^+(k,H_p)$ and $Q^-(k,H_p)$, respectively.*\n\n3.  *Finite initial portions of these two arms have all their vertices in the same ball of radius $R$, and the sum of the increases along those portions is at least $b>0$.*\n\n4.  *The base rays from $y$ of labels $\\alpha$ and $-\\beta$ are contained in $Q^+(y,H_c)$ and $Q^-(y,H_c)$, respectively.*\n\n\n*There is a separation $S=S(s,R,H_p,H_c)$ such that $y_1-k_1>S$ implies $$\\label{eq:budget-comparison}\n b\\le B_\\alpha(y,k)+B_{-\\beta}(y,k).$$ One may take $$\\label{eq:budget-separation}\n S=\\max\\left\\{R+\\frac{R+H_c}{s},\\frac{H_p+H_c}{s}\\right\\}\n \\le C_s(1+R+H_p+H_c),$$ with $C_s$ depending only on $s$. In particular, the choice of $S$ is uniform over bins and does not depend on the magnitudes of the individual increases.",
        "source_id": "PM-212.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-label-budget",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a bin $(\\alpha,\\beta)_I$, an integer spacing $l\\ge1$, and a gain $b>0$. On a common probability space, mark some sites of the horizontal grid $k_j=(jl,0)$, $j\\in\\mathbb Z$. Suppose all sites have the same marginal probability $q_{\\alpha,\\beta}$ of being marked in this bin, and the base environment has its usual fixed-label Busemann asymptotics. Suppose also that for successive marks $k<y$ in the bin the comparison <a href=\"#eq:budget-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:budget-comparison\">[eq:budget-comparison]</a> holds. In particular, this is so if the hypotheses of [lem:budget-comparison](#lem:budget-comparison) hold with common deterministic bounds and successive marks are sufficiently separated. Then $$\\label{eq:label-budget}\n \\frac{b}{l}\\,q_{\\alpha,\\beta}\n \\le r(\\beta)-r(\\alpha).$$ No independence or ergodicity assumption on the marks is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-212.1"
      },

Then $$\\label{eq:label-budget}\n \\frac{b}{l}\\,q_{\\alpha,\\beta}\n \\le r(\\beta)-r(\\alpha).$$ No independence or ergodicity assumption on the marks is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-212.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-usable",
        "exact_quote": "There exist $a_0<b_0$ such that $$\\label{eq:low-usable-probabilities}\n \\begin{gathered}\n p_a=\\mathbb P(t_e\\le a_0)>0,\n \\qquad q=\\mathbb P(t_e<b_0)<\\frac1{64},\\\\\n p_b=\\mathbb P(t_e\\ge b_0)=1-q>0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Call an edge *usable* if $t_e\\ge b_0$, and put $\\theta=8\\sqrt q<1$. For each vertex $x$ and integer $n_0\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:usable-fixed-site}\n \\mathbb P\\left(\n \\begin{array}{c}\n \\text{some simple path from $x$ of length $j\\ge n_0$}\\\\\n \\text{has fewer than $j/2$ usable edges}\n \\end{array}\\right)\n \\le\\frac{\\theta^{n_0}}{1-\\theta}.$$ If $U_n$ is the event that every simple path starting in $[-3n,3n]^2\\cap\\mathbb Z^2$ and having length at least $n/2$ has at least half its edges usable, then $$\\label{eq:usable-box}\n \\mathbb P(U_n^c)\n \\le (6n+1)^2\\frac{\\theta^{\\lceil n/2\\rceil}}{1-\\theta}\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ Almost surely, at every fixed vertex all sufficiently long simple paths satisfy the same fraction bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-212.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-usable",
          "exact_quote": "There exist $a_0<b_0$ such that $$\\label{eq:low-usable-probabilities}\n \\begin{gathered}\n p_a=\\mathbb P(t_e\\le a_0)>0,\n \\qquad q=\\mathbb P(t_e<b_0)<\\frac1{64},\\\\\n p_b=\\mathbb P(t_e\\ge b_0)=1-q>0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Call an edge *usable* if $t_e\\ge b_0$, and put $\\theta=8\\sqrt q<1$. For each vertex $x$ and integer $n_0\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:usable-fixed-site}\n \\mathbb P\\left(\n \\begin{array}{c}\n \\text{some simple path from $x$ of length $j\\ge n_0$}\\\\\n \\text{has fewer than $j/2$ usable edges}\n \\end{array}\\right)\n \\le\\frac{\\theta^{n_0}}{1-\\theta}.$$ If $U_n$ is the event that every simple path starting in $[-3n,3n]^2\\cap\\mathbb Z^2$ and having length at least $n/2$ has at least half its edges usable, then $$\\label{eq:usable-box}\n \\mathbb P(U_n^c)\n \\le (6n+1)^2\\frac{\\theta^{\\lceil n/2\\rceil}}{1-\\theta}\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ Almost surely, at every fixed vertex all sufficiently long simple paths satisfy the same fraction bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-212.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There exist $a_0<b_0$ such that $$\\label{eq:low-usable-probabilities}\n \\begin{gathered}\n p_a=\\mathbb P(t_e\\le a_0)>0,\n \\qquad q=\\mathbb P(t_e<b_0)<\\frac1{64},\\\\\n p_b=\\mathbb P(t_e\\ge b_0)=1-q>0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Call an edge *usable* if $t_e\\ge b_0$, and put $\\theta=8\\sqrt q<1$.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-same-busemann",
        "exact_quote": "On one event of probability one, whenever two oriented bigeodesics $p,q$ in any fixed chart have the same upper label, their two Busemann functions agree: $$\\label{eq:same-busemann-pair}\n B_{p^+}=B_{q^+},\\qquad B_{p^-}=B_{q^-}.$$ This assertion is simultaneous over the bigeodesics and their possibly random labels.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-likelihood",
        "exact_quote": "Every individual environment $Y^{k,R}$ and every union environment $Y^{k,y,R}$ has law absolutely continuous with respect to $\\mu$. There is a finite constant $C$, depending only on $s,H_0,p_a,p_b$, such that the density $L_{k,R}=d\\mathcal L(Y^{k,R})/d\\mu$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:likelihood-bound}\n \\mathbb E_\\mu L_{k,R}^2\\leq\\exp(C\\eta^2),\\qquad\n \\|\\mathcal L(Y^{k,R})-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\leq\\tfrac12\\sqrt{\\exp(C\\eta^2)-1}.$$ The bounds are uniform in $k$ and $R$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **finite geodesic** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A finite *geodesic* realizes this infimum.
- **ray** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *ray* $p=(p_0,p_1,\ldots)$ is a simple infinite nearest-neighbor path every finite segment of which is a geodesic.
- **bigeodesic** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *bigeodesic* has the same property and is indexed by $\mathbb Z$.
- **coalescence of rays** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Two rays *coalesce* if, after deleting finite initial portions, their vertex sequences agree.
- **Busemann function and label of a ray** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A ray $p$ has a Busemann function $$B_p(x,y)=\lim_{j\to\infty} \bigl(T(x,p_j)-T(y,p_j)\bigr)$$ with a linear asymptote $\rho_p$, which we call its label.
- **label** ([source](#pm-h-7)): We call $\rho_p$ the *label* of $p$.
- **dual unit circle and contact directions** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The set $\mathcal S$ is the dual unit circle, and $A(\rho)$ is the set of contact directions of $\rho$.
- **offset cones** ([source](#sec:sectors)): For $z\in\mathbb Z^2$ and $H\geq0$, define the closed convex offset cones $$\label{eq:offset-cones} Q^\pm(z,H)= \{w\in\mathbb R^2:\ \pm(w_2-z_2)\geq s\|w_1-z_1\|-H\}.$$
- **exceptional label** ([source](#pm-h-12)): $$ Consequently $B_q(x,w)\le B(x,w)$. Write $\sigma=\rho_q$ and $\rho=\rho_p$. Asymptotic linearity, with the fixed basepoint $x$, and the choices $w=ke_i$ and $w=-ke_i$, $i=1,2$, imply respectively $\sigma(e_i)\le\rho(e_i)$ and $\sigma(e_i)\ge\rho(e_i)$. Thus $\sigma=\rho$. ◻ </details> Call a label $\rho\in I$ *exceptional* in an environment $\omega$ if at least one of the following holds:
- **set of exceptional labels** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Denote this set by $\mathcal E_I(\omega)$.
- **usable edge** ([source](#stmt-lem-usable)): Call an edge *usable* if $t_e\ge b_0$, and put $\theta=8\sqrt q
- **upper fork** ([source](#sec:forks)): An *upper fork at $x$* consists of two bigeodesics, oriented toward upper labels in $I$, with the same upper label, coincident backwards from $x$, and with different first edges forwards from $x$.
- **perturbation region** ([source](#eq:tilt-region)): For a horizontal site $k=(k_1,0)$ and an integer $R\geq1$, define the finite set of edges $$\label{eq:tilt-region} A_k(R)=\left\{e:\ h(e)\leq R,\quad \max_{w\text{ endpoint of }e}\|w_1-k_1\| \leq1+\frac{h(e)+H_0}{s}\right\}.$$
- **replacement scale** ([source](#pm-h-4)): At height $h$, we use the replacement scale $$\delta_h=\frac{\eta}{(h+2)\log(h+2)}$$ with one sufficiently small $\eta>0$.
- **random coalescing geodesic** ([source](#sec:boundary-comparison)): a *random coalescing geodesic* is a measurable choice of a ray from the origin whose lattice translates coalesce almost surely.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-minimum-moment | second moment of the minimum of four independent weights | < | $\infty$ | law G of the edge weights |
| q-cone-offset-growth | maximal cone offset over a ball | = | $o(n)$ | fixed L and n tending to infinity |
| q-budget-separation | required separation for budget comparison | ≤ | $C_s(1+R+H_p+H_c)$ | slope s, radius R and offsets |
| q-label-budget | gain per unit spacing times marking probability | ≤ | $r(\beta)-r(\alpha)$ | bin, spacing and gain |
| q-usable-threshold-mass | probability of weights below the upper threshold | < | $\frac1{64}$ | choice of upper threshold |
| q-edge-label-count | expected number of labels through an edge | ≤ | $C(H+1)$ | every edge, every offset H |
| q-fork-credit | fork count credit lower bound | ≤ | $\frac n4 F_n$ | large n |
| q-fork-probability | probability that the origin is a fork | ≤ | $40C\varepsilon$ | every positive epsilon |
| q-density-second-moment | second moment of the change-of-law density | ≤ | $\exp(C\eta^2)$ | uniform in site and radius |
| q-gain-probability | conditional probability of a raise and large toll | ≥ | $3/4$ | each positive integer M with suitable radius |
| q-mark-probability | marginal probability of a mark | ≥ | $p/4$ | every site and every M |
| q-retained-mark-probability | probability of a retained mark | ≥ | $p/8$ | horizontal sites |
| q-final-partition-budget | probability a site survives all filters | ≤ | $\frac{lW_I}{M}$ | fixed M and partition index |
| q-contradiction-threshold | gain at which the contradiction occurs | > | $8lW_I/p$ | integer M |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-finite-geodesics** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-minimizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-minimizers): Exponential moment path counting plus a probability-zero tie argument -> unique finite geodesics
- **s-busemann-basic** [claim](#stmt-lem-busemann-basic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-busemann-basic): Monotone bounded sequence of passage-time differences -> Busemann function with calibration
- **s-structure** [claim](#stmt-thm-structure) / [proof](#stmt-thm-structure): Imported structural theory with a boundary comparison replacement -> deterministic norm, label set, fixed-label coalescence
- **s-fixed-label** [claim](#stmt-cor-fixed-label) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fixed-label): Backward finiteness contradiction -> no bigeodesic end with a deterministic label
- **s-uniform-metric** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-metric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-metric): Extended shape theorem applied to the farther endpoint -> uniform metric approximation
- **s-finite-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-change): Loop erasure and counting changed edges -> stability of distances and labels
- **s-measurability** [claim](#stmt-lem-measurable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-measurable): Borel tests, analytic projection and Jankov–von Neumann selection -> measurable existence events and selectors
- **s-opposite** [claim](#stmt-lem-opposite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-opposite): Triangle inequality along opposite arms and linear asymptotics -> opposite labels of the two arms
- **s-charts** [claim](#stmt-lem-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-charts): Convex geometry of the dual ball and countable dense selection -> countable chart family
- **s-ray-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-ray-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ray-order): Jordan curve strip-crossing argument on near-calibrated walks -> stable ray order compatible with labels
- **s-cone** [claim](#stmt-prop-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cone): Trap intermediate rays between four fixed bounding rays by a first-arrival argument -> uniform cone confinement
- **s-crossing** [claim](#stmt-lem-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-crossing): Last visits to a row and the order contradiction -> intersection of reverse-ordered rays
- **s-calibrated** [claim](#stmt-lem-calibrated-rays) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-calibrated-rays): Diagonal limit of finite geodesics toward a ray -> calibrated ray with the same label
- **s-exceptional** [claim](#stmt-prop-exceptional-countable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exceptional-countable): Planar word argument for branching and disjoint value intervals for Busemann disagreement -> countable exceptional label set
- **s-independent** [claim](#stmt-lem-independent-exceptional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-independent-exceptional): Analytic bad set and Fubini over independent environments -> coalescence of opposite rays in an independent environment
- **s-cut-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-cut-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cut-transfer): Moving-cut barrier estimates and last-cut comparison -> ray and label transfer across a horizontal cut
- **s-opposite-coalescence** [claim](#stmt-prop-opposite-coalescence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-opposite-coalescence): Coupling of two half-plane environments -> opposite coalescence at exceptional labels
- **s-budget-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-budget-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-budget-comparison): Bypass through base comparator rays after a finite increase -> bound of the gain by Busemann increments
- **s-label-budget** [claim](#stmt-prop-label-budget) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-label-budget): Telescoping over marks and reverse Fatou -> gain times marking probability bounded by label width
- **s-usable** [claim](#stmt-lem-usable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-usable): Union bound over paths and edge subsets -> many usable edges on long simple paths
- **s-edge-count** [claim](#stmt-prop-edge-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-edge-count): One-edge resampling marks and the label budget summed over bins -> linear bound on labels through an edge
- **s-no-forks** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-forks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-forks): Finite tree leaf count and density versus boundary comparison -> absence of forks
- **s-same-busemann** [claim](#stmt-cor-same-busemann) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-same-busemann): Differing Busemann functions would create a fork -> equal Busemann pairs for equal labels
- **s-likelihood** [claim](#stmt-lem-likelihood) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-likelihood): Product of one-edge densities and a convergent series -> uniform change-of-law bound
- **s-posterior** [claim](#stmt-lem-posterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-posterior): Joint law computation and monotone class extension -> conditional independent Bernoulli raises
- **s-divergent-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-divergent-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-divergent-gain): Summation by parts and Chebyshev bounds -> large toll with a near raise
- **s-tilt-marks** [claim](#stmt-prop-tilt-marks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tilt-marks): Total variation transfer and the divergent gain lemma -> marks with bounded-below probability
- **s-union** [claim](#stmt-lem-union) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-union): Edgewise weight comparison and spacing of regions -> marked paths persist in the union metric
- **s-distinct-marks** [claim](#stmt-prop-distinct-marks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-distinct-marks): Detour through a raised edge compared with a selected geodesic -> distinct labels at marked sites
- **s-boundary-correction** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-correction): Monotone calibrated limits for restricted and full metrics -> stationary boundary correction
- **s-zero-boundary** [claim](#stmt-lem-zero-boundary-difference) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-zero-boundary-difference): Truncation, stationarity and bounded convergence -> vanishing increments
- **s-boundary-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-boundary-uniqueness): Swapped crossing walks with nonnegative excess and ergodicity -> uniqueness of coalescing families per functional
- **s-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-distinct-marks-2): Contradiction by combining the label budget with marks of arbitrarily large gain -> almost sure absence of bigeodesics

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-no-bigeodesic-as**: For a nonatomic edge law whose minimum of four independent weights has finite second moment, one probability-one event contains no bigeodesic of the planar first-passage metric. The conclusion is simultaneous over all candidate paths and all end directions.
- **inv-label-budget**: On a horizontal grid with fixed spacing, the common marking probability times the gain per mark, divided by the spacing, is bounded by the label width of the bin. This is the budget that the growing gains eventually contradict.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-atomic-weights**: Apply the no-bigeodesic conclusion to graphs with discrete or atomic edge weights, such as hop-count metrics. -> Ties between distinct paths have positive probability, so finite geodesics are no longer unique and the ray-order and fork arguments lose their basis.
- **anti-nonplanar-lattice**: Transfer the argument to higher-dimensional or non-planar networks. -> The proof depends on planar left-to-right ordering of disjoint rays on horizontal rows, which has no analogue when paths can pass around each other.
- **anti-dependent-delays**: Apply the result to delays that depend on traffic or are correlated between edges. -> The change-of-law and replacement estimates need a product measure over edges, and passage-time additivity of the environment is lost.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main theorem is an almost-sure nonexistence statement about doubly infinite geodesics in a random infinite environment. Its proof is a contradiction argument using measurability, change of law and countable intersections over infinitely many paths and labels. The paper states no finite procedure whose output could be computed and checked, so no runnable reference code or independent oracle exists.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an almost-sure non-existence statement for doubly infinite geodesics. It gives no finite procedure, API or data format to implement. Software would at most simulate finite-box first-passage times, which the paper does not specify. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result describes the random metric on the infinite lattice. A game or simulator can only sample a finite patch, where finite geodesics exist and are almost surely unique, so the result offers no usable simulation rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or certificate procedure comes out of the proof. Every step is an existence, measurability or probabilistic argument about random environments. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no algorithm: the contradiction argument compares marking probabilities and passage-time budgets. Computing geodesics in a finite box is classical shortest-path work and is not supplied by this paper. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite-tree leaf count and the rooted tree of forward paths are proof devices. They do not define a data structure with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns computation, memory or parallel execution. The statements are asymptotic and probabilistic on an infinite lattice. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no security notion, hardness assumption or adversary. Random edge weights serve only as a percolation model. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No loss, learning rule or model class appears. The change-of-law estimates for perturbed edge weights are used only to bound probabilities in the contradiction. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Geodesics are minimizers of path cost, but the result is qualitative: there is no optimisation problem to solve and no bound on a solver. Finite minimizers are the ordinary shortest-path problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model has independent random edge weights on a fixed lattice and no communication, failure or consensus model. Routing intuition would only be an analogy, and the result requires undirected independent continuous weights. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural outside use is as an input to formal or probabilistic work on first-passage percolation. That is mathematical reuse, not an engineering construction, so no implementable contract is given. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-nonatomic-law** (hypothesis): The main theorem needs the edge law to have no atoms. With atoms, distinct paths can tie, so finite geodesics need not be unique, and the ordering and fork arguments built on uniqueness no longer apply. Blocked use: ['Applying the no-bigeodesic conclusion to discrete or unit-weight lattice metrics', 'Using uniqueness of finite geodesics for a law with point masses']
- **lim-minimum-moment** (hypothesis): The conclusion is proved only when the minimum of four independent copies of the weight has finite second moment. This is the stated moment class, and the paper does not assert a moment-free version. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to heavy-tailed laws that violate the minimum-of-four condition', 'Citing the theorem as a resolution of the moment-free conjecture']
- **lim-planar-order** (hypothesis): The ray-order lemma, the cone confinement and the crossing lemma use the planar left-to-right order of disjoint rays on horizontal rows, obtained from Jordan curve separation. The whole argument is therefore specific to the planar lattice. Blocked use: ['Transferring the proof to higher-dimensional lattices', 'Using the order argument on non-planar network topologies']
- **lim-fixed-label-null-sets** (formal_scope): The coalescence and backward-finiteness conclusions of the structural input hold for each fixed functional, with a null set that may depend on that functional. They cannot be intersected over all functionals, so they do not directly control a label chosen by a bigeodesic that depends on the environment. Blocked use: ['Treating fixed-label coalescence as simultaneous over all random labels', 'Intersecting the fixed-label events over an uncountable family of functionals']
- **lim-imported-structure** (formal_scope): The structural theorem is imported from earlier work together with the shape theorem. The boundary comparison replaces one expectation step but is itself part of that input, so the theorem inherits the scope of the imported theory. Blocked use: ['Reading the structural theorem as independently proved by the elementary arguments of this paper', 'Dropping the minimum-moment scope of the imported statements']
- **lim-no-rate** (approximation): The confinement offsets are controlled only as a sublinear growth on balls. No rate of Busemann convergence over the intermediate labels is available, so no quantitative statement about how fast rays settle into their cones follows. Blocked use: ['Extracting an explicit convergence rate or finite-size error bound from the cone confinement', 'Using the offset bound as a uniform estimate over all labels']
- **lim-contradiction-setting** (hypothesis): The perturbation construction with divergent gain is carried out under the standing assumption that bigeodesics exist with positive probability in a chart. Its marks and label bounds describe a hypothetical situation used for a contradiction, not objects present in the actual environment. Blocked use: ['Reading the marks and the gains as existing in a real random environment', 'Using the construction as a sampling procedure for bigeodesics']
- **lim-nonconstructive-selection** (nonconstructive): Existence events are analytic and witnesses are chosen by an abstract selection theorem, with no explicit procedure. The proof therefore yields no algorithm that finds or tests for a bigeodesic. Blocked use: ['Implementing the selection step as a computable routine', 'Presenting the result as a decision procedure for bigeodesics in a finite sample']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that planar first-passage percolation with independent identically distributed nonnegative nonatomic edge weights has almost surely no doubly infinite geodesic, provided the minimum of four independent weights has finite second moment. This resolves the planar no-bigeodesics conjecture under that moment assumption. The conclusion rules out all bigeodesics simultaneously, without any regularity assumption on the limit shape.
